See [Set up AI](/organization/ai-providers/). ## Set the copyright header for generated code [Section titled “Set the copyright header for generated code”](#set-the-copyright-header-for-generated-code) Every C#, TypeScript and JavaScript file Studio generates can start with a header, written as comments. Leave it empty and generated files have no header. 1. Select **Code generation**. 2. Write the text under **Header**. Write `{year}` where the year should go; it becomes the year the code is generated. 3. Check the **Preview**, then select **Save**. app.cratis.studio ![The Code generation page with the Copyright header field and its preview](/_astro/settings-code-generation.DgafxZsT_1AnTJe.webp) Agents that fill in the rest of the code are told to use the same header. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Set up AI](/organization/ai-providers/) * [Generate code](/deliver/generate-code/)

# Connect GitHub

> Install the Studio GitHub App on the GitHub organizations or accounts your code lives in, so projects get repositories, pull requests, builds, Git sync and issues.

GitHub is where Studio puts the code it generates and where it keeps your work in step with your team. Your organization connects GitHub once by installing the **Studio GitHub App** on the GitHub organizations or accounts your code lives in. After that, every project can choose a repository from those installations. With GitHub connected, a project can: * **Publish generated code** as branches and pull requests, instead of keeping it in Studio’s local repository. See [Generate code](/deliver/generate-code/). * **Let builders work in the repository**, whether Studio handles the branches and pull requests or the builder does. See [Builders](/developers/builders/). * **Keep a board in sync with Git**, so the model lives beside the code. See [Keep a board in sync with Git](/deliver/git-sync/). * **Create issues from slices** and follow their state, and keep slice status and issue state in step. See [Link slices to issues](/deliver/issue-links/). No personal token is kept. Whenever Studio needs to work in a repository, it asks GitHub for a short-lived token for that one repository. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * You are a maintainer of the organization. * You can install GitHub Apps on the GitHub organization or account your code lives in, or you can ask its owner to approve the installation. ## Connect GitHub [Section titled “Connect GitHub”](#connect-github) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Integrations**. 2. Under **Source Control**, select **Connect GitHub**. 3. On GitHub, choose the GitHub organization or account your code lives in, choose which repositories the app can reach (all of them, or only the ones you select), and install the app. 4. GitHub sends you back to Studio. The installation is listed under **Source Control**. If it is your organization’s first installation, it becomes the one **Projects use by default**. app.cratis.studio ![Organization settings, Integrations: the Source Control section with its GitHub installations above Issues, Other providers and Modeling tools](/_astro/settings-integrations.Dwj_4k98_Z1RJNJn.webp) GitHub installations are listed under Source Control, at the top of Integrations. If **Connect GitHub** is not offered, the page says *The Studio GitHub App is not configured for this Studio, so GitHub cannot be connected.* Contact support (see [Get help](/reference/get-help/)). ## Several GitHub organizations [Section titled “Several GitHub organizations”](#several-github-organizations) Connect GitHub again for each GitHub organization or account your code lives in. Each installation is listed on its own. **Projects use by default** says which one a new project starts with; a project can choose any of them. Which repositories an installation can reach is decided on GitHub. To give Studio another repository, change the installation’s repository access on GitHub. ## Check that an installation is ready [Section titled “Check that an installation is ready”](#check-that-an-installation-is-ready) Each installation shows whether Studio can use it: | Status | What it means | | ------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Ready** | Studio can use it for everything. | | **Waiting for approval on GitHub** | The app asks for a permission, such as **Issues**, that the owner has not accepted yet. It keeps working for source control. Select **Review and approve on GitHub** to send the owner there. | | **Unavailable** | Studio cannot reach it, for example because the app was suspended or uninstalled on GitHub. | | *The provider could not be asked just now* | GitHub did not answer. Studio asks again later. | ## Give a project its repository [Section titled “Give a project its repository”](#give-a-project-its-repository) An installation only makes repositories available. Each project chooses its own on **Project Settings** > **Source control**: pick the **Installation**, then **Choose the repository**, then **Connect repository**. An application can use a different repository from the rest of its project. app.cratis.studio ![Application settings, Source control: the installation and repository the application publishes generated code to](/_astro/application-settings-source-control.DoGIj8Ch_Z25XQx2.webp) A project or application chooses one repository from the installations your organization connected. The project’s source control also says whether the repository is ready: * *Ready to publish generated branches.* Everything works. * *This installation needs to be reapproved on GitHub before Studio can publish generated branches.* The app asks for more permissions than the owner has granted. An owner of the GitHub organization approves them on GitHub; select **Review on GitHub**. * *Studio cannot read this installation’s settings on GitHub*, so it cannot tell whether the installation grants what code generation needs. * *The installation cannot reach any repository.* Choose which repositories the app can reach on GitHub. * *This installation is no longer available on GitHub.* Connect the repository again. The steps are in [Connect a repository](/deliver/connect-a-repository/). ## Send issues to GitHub [Section titled “Send issues to GitHub”](#send-issues-to-github) Issues go to the same place as source control unless you choose otherwise. Under **Issues**, **Issues go to** is **The same as source control** by default, which sends slice issues to the project’s GitHub repository. You can pick another GitHub installation, or another issue tracker such as [Jira](/organization/jira/) or [Linear](/organization/linear/). To create issues, the installation needs the **Issues** permission. An installation that shows **Waiting for approval on GitHub** can’t create issues until the owner approves it. ## Remove an installation [Section titled “Remove an installation”](#remove-an-installation) Select **Remove** next to the installation. An installation chosen for source control or issues has to be replaced there first: Studio says *Choose another installation for source control or issues first.* Removing it means projects can no longer choose its repositories. Repositories projects already use keep working, and issues already linked stay linked. Removing it in Studio does not uninstall the app. To take its access away, uninstall the Studio GitHub App in the GitHub organization’s or account’s settings. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) [Connect a repository ](/deliver/connect-a-repository/)Give a project the repository generated code goes to. [Keep a board in sync with Git ](/deliver/git-sync/)Keep the model beside the code. [Link slices to issues ](/deliver/issue-links/)Create issues from slices and follow them. [Connect integrations ](/organization/integrations/)Everything else your organization can connect.

# Connect GitLab and Azure DevOps

> Connect GitLab or Azure DevOps to link, create and close issues from slices, and see what Studio can and cannot do with them yet.

Studio connects your organization to GitLab and Azure DevOps through its own apps. Nobody pastes a personal access token. Today they serve **issues**; Studio’s repository work, such as generated pull requests, runs against GitHub only. ## What works today [Section titled “What works today”](#what-works-today) | | GitLab | Azure DevOps | | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | | Connect from **Settings** > **Integrations** | Yes | Yes | | Issues: link, create, read the state, close and reopen | GitLab issues | Azure Boards work items | | A project names its repository | Projects you can push to | Git repositories in every Azure DevOps organization you belong to | | Generated code, merge requests, webhooks, Git sync and builds | Not yet | Not yet | Source control is limited for now A GitLab or Azure DevOps connection chosen for source control shows **Limited**, with the reason *Repository operations are not yet available*. A project that names its repository there shows the same notice in **Project Settings** > **Source control**, and works like a project without a connected repository for everything Studio does with Git. ## Connect [Section titled “Connect”](#connect) You need to be a maintainer. 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Integrations**. 2. Under **Other providers**, select **Connect GitLab** or **Connect Azure DevOps**. 3. The provider asks you to sign in and allow Studio’s app. 4. The browser comes back to the Integrations page with the outcome. The connection is named after the person who allowed the app (their GitLab username, or their Azure DevOps display name), and Studio acts as that person. When your organization has not chosen where issues go, the first connection you make becomes the place. You can remove a connection once nothing uses it. If a provider shows *Not configured by the Studio operator*, its app is not set up for this Studio yet, and there is no **Connect** button for it. Contact support (see [Get help](/reference/get-help/)). app.cratis.studio ![Organization settings, Integrations, scrolled to Other providers: GitLab, Azure DevOps, Jira and Linear each read Not configured by the Studio operator](/_astro/integrations-other-providers.Dt1RSyX-_2pufoW.webp) Under Other providers, each provider shows Not configured by the Studio operator when its app is not set up for this Studio. Nothing can be connected until it is. ## Write issue references [Section titled “Write issue references”](#write-issue-references) * **GitLab:** an issue is written `group/project#17`. Subgroups are allowed (`group/subgroup/project#17`). A project’s issue destination is a GitLab project, `group/project`. * **Azure DevOps:** a work item is written `organization/project#42`. A project’s issue destination is `organization/project`. Studio creates the first of *User Story*, *Product Backlog Item*, *Requirement*, *Issue* or *Task* that the project’s process offers, and closes or reopens a work item by moving it to its type’s first *Completed* or *Proposed* state. Pasting the address of a GitLab issue or an Azure DevOps work item into an issue link reads it as such. See [Link slices to issues](/deliver/issue-links/). ## When a connection stops working [Section titled “When a connection stops working”](#when-a-connection-stops-working) Tokens are encrypted and renewed by Studio. When one cannot be renewed (the person left, or revoked Studio’s access), the connection shows **Unavailable** and asks you to connect it again. An Azure DevOps organization whose policy does not allow third-party applications is left out of the repository list. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/) * [Project and application settings](/organization/project-and-application-settings/)

# Connect integrations

> Connect GitHub, Jira, Linear, GitLab, Azure DevOps and Miro to your organization once, so every project can use them.

Studio works with the services your team already uses: where your code lives, where issues go and the modeling tools you draw in. You connect each one **once for the organization**. Projects then choose from what is connected; nobody connects them project by project. You need to be a maintainer. Open **Organization settings** and select **Integrations**. app.cratis.studio ![The Integrations page with Source Control, Issues and Other providers sections](/_astro/settings-integrations.Dwj_4k98_Z1RJNJn.webp) Source control, issues, other providers and modeling tools, all in one place. The page has these sections: | Section | What it holds | | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Source Control** | The GitHub installations of your organization and which one projects use by default | | **Issues** | Where the issues Studio creates for slices go | | **Other providers** | Jira, Linear, GitLab and Azure DevOps | | **Modeling tools** | Miro | [GitHub ](/organization/github/)Repositories, pull requests, builds, Git sync and issues. [Jira ](/organization/jira/)Send slice issues to Jira Cloud. [Linear ](/organization/linear/)Send slice issues to a Linear workspace. [GitLab and Azure DevOps ](/organization/gitlab-and-azure-devops/)Issues and work items, and what works today. [Miro ](/organization/miro/)Import and draw boards on Miro. ## Choose where issues go [Section titled “Choose where issues go”](#choose-where-issues-go) Under **Issues**, pick where slice issues are created with **Issues go to**. The default is **The same as source control**. You can choose another GitHub installation, a Jira site, a Linear workspace, a GitLab project’s connection or an Azure DevOps one. When you have never chosen, the first Jira site or Linear workspace you connect becomes the place issues go. Slices create issues, show the state of their issues and can keep their status and the issue’s in step. How to link and create them is in [Link slices to issues](/deliver/issue-links/). ## Remove a connection [Section titled “Remove a connection”](#remove-a-connection) Select **Remove** on an installation, site or workspace. If it is chosen for source control or issues, Studio says *Choose somewhere else for issues first* (or *Choose another installation for source control or issues first*). Removing it does not uninstall Studio’s app from the other service. Repositories projects already use keep working, and issues already linked stay linked. To take the app’s access away, remove it in GitHub, Jira, Linear or wherever you installed it. ## Connections made with a token earlier [Section titled “Connections made with a token earlier”](#connections-made-with-a-token-earlier) Issue trackers connected with a token before these integrations existed are listed under **Connected with a token (legacy)** with the note *Legacy - reconnect through the app*. They keep working until you disconnect them, and once a Jira site or Linear workspace is connected through the app, Studio uses the app. Nothing new can be connected with a token. ## What projects do next [Section titled “What projects do next”](#what-projects-do-next) A project chooses its repository on **Project Settings** > **Source control** from the installations you connected, and its issue destination under **Issues**. See [Project and application settings](/organization/project-and-application-settings/). ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Project and application settings](/organization/project-and-application-settings/) * [Keep a board in sync with Git](/deliver/git-sync/) * [Webhooks](/organization/webhooks/)

# Invite people and manage members

> Invite a colleague to your organization, send the invitation again, and remove people or take back their access.

You add people to your organization by inviting them by email. Everyone you invite signs in with the identity provider they prefer, so they do not need a new password. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * You are a maintainer of the organization. See [Roles and permissions](/organization/roles-and-permissions/). * A free [seat](/organization/seats/) is helpful, but not required to send the invitation. ## Invite someone [Section titled “Invite someone”](#invite-someone) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Users**. 2. Select **Invite**. The **Invite User** dialog opens. app.cratis.studio ![The Invite User dialog with an Email field, a Role field set to Member, and the Invite and Cancel buttons](/_astro/invite-user-dialog.BUOdHqgU_2j8wyJ.webp) Enter the address and pick a role. 3. Enter the person’s address under **Email**. 4. Choose a **Role**: **Member** or **Maintainer**. If you are unsure, start with **Member**; you can [make them a maintainer](/organization/roles-and-permissions/#make-someone-a-maintainer) later. 5. Select **Invite**. Studio emails the person a link. If no seat is free, the dialog says so: *No seats are available in your organization. This user will not be able to use Studio when they sign in until a seat frees up or more are added.* You can still invite them; [add seats](/organization/billing-and-subscription/) or free one before they arrive. ## What the invited person does [Section titled “What the invited person does”](#what-the-invited-person-does) They follow the link in the email, sign in with any provider Studio offers, accept the terms and the privacy policy, and join your organization. The person can belong to several organizations and switches between them from the **Account** menu with **Switch organization**. ## Follow up on invitations [Section titled “Follow up on invitations”](#follow-up-on-invitations) Until an invitation is accepted, the person appears in the list with the status **Invited**, and under **Pending invitations**. In the person’s **Actions**: * **Refresh invitation** sends them a fresh link, for when the first one expired or got lost. * **Revoke invitation** takes the invitation back after you confirm. app.cratis.studio ![The Users page listing members and agents, with the status column and the seat counter](/_astro/settings-users.CUABBLwi_ZnzPYB.webp) The list shows each person's role and status. Names and addresses are blurred. ## Manage the people who have joined [Section titled “Manage the people who have joined”](#manage-the-people-who-have-joined) Each row shows **Email**, **Role**, **Name** and **Status**. A person’s status is **Member**; Studio’s own AI agents are listed too, with the status **Agent**. In a person’s **Actions** you can: * **Make maintainer** to give them the maintainer role. * **Assign seat** or **Unassign seat**. See [Seats](/organization/seats/). * **Project access** to see which projects they can reach and **Revoke access** to one of them. The person can no longer open that project until access is granted again. * **Remove** to take them out of the organization after you confirm. ## Check that it worked [Section titled “Check that it worked”](#check-that-it-worked) The person appears in the list with the status **Invited**, and turns into **Member** once they have accepted. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Seats](/organization/seats/) * [Roles and permissions](/organization/roles-and-permissions/) * [Sign up and start your trial](/get-started/sign-up-and-trial/)

# Connect Jira

> Authorize Studio's Jira app on your Jira Cloud sites so slices can create issues and follow their state.

Your organization sends the issues Studio creates for slices to Jira Cloud by letting Studio’s own Jira app work on your Jira sites. No personal API token is kept. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * You are a maintainer of the organization. * You can use or administer the Jira site you want to connect. ## Connect a site [Section titled “Connect a site”](#connect-a-site) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Integrations**. 2. Under **Other providers**, select **Connect Jira**. 3. Atlassian asks you to allow Studio’s app on a Jira site. Allow it. 4. Atlassian sends you back to the Integrations page, which says *Jira is connected.* Every Jira site the authorization reaches is connected and named by its address, such as `acme.atlassian.net`. Connect again, for example with another Atlassian account, to add the sites that account reaches. If Jira shows *Not configured by the Studio operator*, Studio’s Jira app is not set up for this Studio and nothing can be connected yet. Contact support (see [Get help](/reference/get-help/)). app.cratis.studio ![Organization settings, Integrations, Other providers: Jira, with GitLab, Azure DevOps and Linear, reads Not configured by the Studio operator](/_astro/integrations-other-providers.Dt1RSyX-_2pufoW.webp) Jira appears under Other providers. When it reads Not configured by the Studio operator, as every provider does here, there is no Connect Jira button. ## Use it for issues [Section titled “Use it for issues”](#use-it-for-issues) When your organization has never chosen where issues go, the first site you connect becomes the place. Otherwise **Issues** keeps its choice and the site is offered there. Choose it under **Issues** > **Issues go to**. See [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/#choose-where-issues-go). Each site shows whether it is ready. It is while the authorization still reaches it. A site Studio’s app can no longer reach, because the app was removed in Jira or your access ended, shows the reason and asks you to connect Jira again. ## What Studio is allowed to do [Section titled “What Studio is allowed to do”](#what-studio-is-allowed-to-do) Studio asks to read and write Jira work: to create issues, read their state and move them through the workflow. It also asks for a refresh token so it can keep working without you signing in again. Tokens are encrypted with your organization’s own key and are only decrypted at the moment they are handed to Atlassian. ## Disconnect [Section titled “Disconnect”](#disconnect) Select **Remove** next to the site. A site chosen for issues has to be replaced there first. Removing it does not take Studio’s app off the site; do that in Jira’s or your Atlassian account’s settings for connected apps. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/) * [Link slices to issues](/deliver/issue-links/)

# Connect Linear

> Authorize Studio's Linear app on your workspace so slices can create issues and move them between states.

Your organization sends the issues Studio creates for slices to Linear by letting Studio’s own Linear app work in your workspace. No personal API key is kept, and issues are created by the app rather than by the person who connected it. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) You are a maintainer of the organization and can authorize apps in the Linear workspace. ## Connect a workspace [Section titled “Connect a workspace”](#connect-a-workspace) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Integrations**. 2. Under **Other providers**, select **Connect Linear**. 3. Linear asks you to allow Studio’s app on a workspace. Allow it. 4. Linear sends you back to the Integrations page, which says *Linear is connected.* The workspace is listed under its own name. Select **Connect another Linear** to connect another workspace. If Linear shows *Not configured by the Studio operator*, Studio’s Linear app is not set up for this Studio and nothing can be connected yet. Contact support (see [Get help](/reference/get-help/)). app.cratis.studio ![Organization settings, Integrations, Other providers: Linear, with GitLab, Azure DevOps and Jira, reads Not configured by the Studio operator](/_astro/integrations-other-providers.Dt1RSyX-_2pufoW.webp) Linear is the last entry under Other providers. When it reads Not configured by the Studio operator, there is no Connect Linear button. ## Use it for issues [Section titled “Use it for issues”](#use-it-for-issues) When your organization has never chosen where issues go, the workspace becomes the place. Otherwise **Issues** keeps its choice and the workspace is offered there. See [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/#choose-where-issues-go). Each workspace shows whether it is ready: it is while Studio’s app can still reach it. If the app was removed in Linear, the workspace shows the reason and asks you to connect Linear again. ## What Studio is allowed to do [Section titled “What Studio is allowed to do”](#what-studio-is-allowed-to-do) Studio asks for read and write access, so it can find a team by its key, create issues and move them between states. Its tokens are encrypted with your organization’s own key and are only decrypted at the moment they are handed to Linear. ## Disconnect [Section titled “Disconnect”](#disconnect) Select **Remove** next to the workspace. A workspace chosen for issues has to be replaced there first. Removing it does not uninstall Studio’s app; do that in Linear’s own settings for applications. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/) * [Link slices to issues](/deliver/issue-links/)

# Issue an MCP credential

> Give your organization an MCP credential so an AI agent can connect to Studio, and rotate it when it should stop working.

An AI agent can work with your organization over the Model Context Protocol (MCP). It can read and change your projects, event models, brainstorming boards and concepts, and only this organization’s. Before it can, a maintainer issues the organization’s credential. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) You are a maintainer. An organization has one MCP credential. If the page says *No MCP server is deployed for this environment yet.*, MCP is not available for this Studio. ## Issue the credential [Section titled “Issue the credential”](#issue-the-credential) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **MCP**. 2. Select **Issue credential**. 3. The page now shows the **Endpoint**, the **Client ID** and the **Client secret**. Select **Show** to read the secret, and **Hide** to cover it again. app.cratis.studio ![The MCP page of Organization settings with the endpoint and client ID, and the secret hidden](/_astro/settings-mcp.otjaDdXm_ZOzRQw.webp) The endpoint and the client ID. The secret stays hidden until you select Show. If issuing fails, the page says nothing was changed. Try again; if it keeps failing, the organization’s encryption key may not be ready yet. Caution Treat the client secret like a password. Anyone who has it can change your boards. Do not paste it into chats, tickets or shared documents. ## Connect the agent [Section titled “Connect the agent”](#connect-the-agent) **How to connect** on the same page lists the steps. The full walk-through, with examples for common agents, is in [Connect an AI agent over MCP](/developers/mcp/). ## Rotate the secret [Section titled “Rotate the secret”](#rotate-the-secret) Select **Rotate secret** and confirm. A new secret is generated at once and the current one stops working, so every agent using it has to be reconfigured. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Connect an AI agent over MCP](/developers/mcp/) * [MCP security for maintainers](/developers/mcp-security/)

# Connect Miro

> Let your organization import event models from Miro and draw boards on Miro, with Studio's Miro app or a token of your own.

Before anyone on your team can import a Miro board or draw a board on Miro, a maintainer connects the organization to Miro once. The token is encrypted with the organization’s own key, is only decrypted at the moment it is handed to Miro, and is never shown again. ## Connect with Studio’s Miro app [Section titled “Connect with Studio’s Miro app”](#connect-with-studios-miro-app) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Integrations**. 2. Under **Modeling tools**, select **Connect to Miro**. 3. In Miro, choose the team and allow the app. 4. You come back to the Integrations page. It says *Connected to Miro with Studio’s Miro app*, with the date. If Miro’s app hands out expiring tokens, Studio renews the access token when it next uses Miro. If renewal fails, you are asked to connect again with **Connect to Miro again**. If **Connect to Miro** is not offered, Studio’s Miro app is not set up for this Studio. Use a token of your own, below, or contact support (see [Get help](/reference/get-help/)). ## Connect with a token of your own [Section titled “Connect with a token of your own”](#connect-with-a-token-of-your-own) Use this when Studio’s app is not available, or you prefer it. 1. In Miro’s developer settings, create an app and install it on your team with the `boards:read` scope. Add `boards:write` if you want to draw boards on Miro. 2. In Studio, open **Settings** > **Integrations** and, under **Modeling tools**, paste the access token into **Miro access token**. 3. Select **Save token**. app.cratis.studio ![Organization settings, Integrations, scrolled to Modeling tools: Miro with the text No Miro access token is configured, the Miro access token box and a Save token button](/_astro/integrations-other-providers.Dt1RSyX-_2pufoW.webp) Under Modeling tools, Miro says No Miro access token is configured until you save one. Paste the token into Miro access token, then select Save token. The page then says *A Miro access token is configured*. **Replace the Miro access token** changes it, and **Remove token** disconnects. Boards already imported stay as they are. ## What your team can do now [Section titled “What your team can do now”](#what-your-team-can-do-now) * **Import from Miro** brings a board into Studio as an event model: see [Import a model](/discover/import-a-model/). * **Draw on Miro** puts a board on Miro, a frame per slice: see [Draw a board on Miro](/share/miro/). Import and draw are one-off. Studio does not keep a Miro board and a Studio board in sync as either changes. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/)

# Project, application and domain settings

> Rename or protect a project, choose its repository and issues, switch AI on per application, set reviewers and more.

Organization settings cover the whole team. A project, an application and a domain each have settings of their own. They sit at the level they describe: a project’s repository on the project, whether AI is used on the application, a folder on the domain. Open them from the gear next to the project, application or domain in the project drawer. Project settings are also under **Project options** > **Project Settings**. ## Project settings [Section titled “Project settings”](#project-settings) app.cratis.studio ![Project Settings of Acme Commerce with the Settings, Reviewers, AI, Source control and Dangerous tabs](/_astro/project-settings.CCIPh7aL_1GSGNx.webp) Project Settings of the Acme Commerce sample. | Tab | What you set | | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Settings** | The **Description** of the project. The name is read-only here; it is renamed on the **Dangerous** tab. | | **Reviewers** | Who reviews slices across the whole project. See below. | | **AI** | **Run autonomously**: Studio runs the workflow on its own. A slice whose analysis comes back sound, with nothing to improve, is assigned to the code generation agent and marked ready for implementation. It needs agents with the *Code generation* and *Analysis* specializations, each with a provider and model. | | **Source control** | The **Source control provider**, **GitHub** or **None (local repository only)**. With GitHub, choose the **Installation** and the **Repository** from [what the organization connected](/organization/integrations/), then **Connect repository**. The status says *Ready to publish generated branches.* or tells you what to approve on GitHub (**Review on GitHub**). You can also name a GitLab or Azure DevOps repository. | | **Issues** | Where the project’s issues go: **The same as source control**, or **A repository of its own** (an **Installation** and a repository as `owner/repository`). | | **Dangerous** | Rename, delete and protect the project. | ### Rename, delete and protect a project [Section titled “Rename, delete and protect a project”](#rename-delete-and-protect-a-project) On **Dangerous**: * **Rename** changes the name everywhere in Studio. It must not be used by another project. * **Delete project** removes the project and everything under it: applications, domains, event models and brainstorming boards. There is no tool to restore it. You confirm by typing the project’s name. * **Protected** stops the project being renamed or deleted, and stops any of its applications being deleted, until someone lifts the protection by typing the project’s name. Fill in **Reason** before turning it on; it is shown to whoever is refused. Only a maintainer can protect a project or lift it. app.cratis.studio ![The Dangerous tab of a protected project, saying it is protected as a reference sample and offering Lift protection](/_astro/project-settings-dangerous.CGGR9A3i_1gTPiN.webp) The Acme Commerce sample is protected, so it cannot be renamed or removed. ### Choose reviewers [Section titled “Choose reviewers”](#choose-reviewers) On the **Reviewers** tab, select who reviews slices. A level can **Add these reviewers** to those configured above it, or **Review with these reviewers instead**. With nobody selected, reviewers are taken from the level above. The same tab exists on applications and domains. app.cratis.studio ![The Reviewers tab of the Acme Sales application settings with the choice between adding to the project's reviewers or replacing them; the reviewers' names are blurred](/_astro/application-settings-reviewers.C97YgwF0_Z1kaCTe.webp) An application can add to the project's reviewers or replace them. Names are blurred. ## Application settings [Section titled “Application settings”](#application-settings) An application’s settings open as a full-screen **Application Settings** panel. app.cratis.studio ![Application Settings of Acme Sales with its tabs from Source control to Dangerous](/_astro/application-settings.wx6p88Vc_ZaSglc.webp) The tabs of Application Settings. | Tab | What you set | | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Source control** | **Use the project’s repository** or **Use a separate repository** for this application. | | **Authentication** | How people sign in to the published application. Claim the published name first, then under **Add a provider** choose **Microsoft Entra ID**, **Google**, **GitHub** or **Auth0, Okta, Keycloak or other OpenID Connect**. Publishing needs a provider whose sign-in test has passed. | | **Publication** | The published name and run options for [publishing the application](/deliver/publish-and-feedback/). | | **Roles** | The application’s own roles, such as *SalesManager*, with **Add Role**. Personas, policies and specifications use them. These are not Studio organization roles. | | **Identity** | What your application knows about a caller beyond the token: **Add detail**, each with a type and one source, a claim or a query. | | **Repository** | The **Base path**: the folder of the application inside the project repository. The preview shows the resolved path. | | **Implementation** | The **Implementation mode**: **Screenplay only**, **Static code**, **Static code + AI** or **AI only**; **Reaction batches**; and **When a built slice changes**. | | **Building** | Which builder builds this application’s slices, and who owns the repository work. | | **AI** | **Enable AI**. Off by default. | | **Predictive modeling** | **Enable predictive modeling**. Needs a configured predictive-modeling agent. | | **Issue status sync** | **Keep slices and their issues in step**. | | **Reviewers** | Who reviews slices in this application. | | **Dangerous** | Rename or delete the application. | Two switches for AI **Enable AI** is off by default and turns on AI in slice work for this application: assigning slices to AI agents, the implementation modes that involve AI and every AI button. It works only once [agents have a provider](/organization/agents-and-skills/). Members who are not maintainers cannot turn AI on; the first-run card tells them to ask a maintainer. app.cratis.studio ![The AI tab of the Acme Sales application settings with the Enable AI switch](/_astro/application-settings-ai.BTzyNSY5_Z2nuj0O.webp) Enable AI is a switch of each application. ### Sign-in and identity [Section titled “Sign-in and identity”](#sign-in-and-identity) app.cratis.studio ![The Authentication tab of the Acme Sales application settings with the Claim published name button and the providers to add](/_astro/application-settings-authentication.BF3TZ8fW_Z7u8UR.webp) Claim the published name, then add a sign-in provider. On **Identity**, **Add detail** to say what your application knows about a caller beyond the token. Each detail has a type and one source: a claim or a query. app.cratis.studio ![The Identity tab of the Acme Sales application settings with the Carried by the token section and the Add detail button](/_astro/application-settings-identity.DCND44DI_Zaw9S9.webp) Identity details the application knows about a caller. ### Implementation modes [Section titled “Implementation modes”](#implementation-modes) | Mode | What happens when a slice is marked ready for implementation | | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | **Screenplay only** | The Screenplay is the deliverable. Studio exports and maintains it and generates no target code. | | **Static code** | Studio renders the deterministic code. No AI agent takes part, and required gaps block instead of being filled in. | | **Static code + AI** | Studio renders the code and the assigned AI agent refines it within the rendered shapes. Needs AI enabled. | | **AI only** | The assigned AI agent implements from the Screenplay and model context alone. Not deterministic. Needs AI enabled. | The mode is captured when a run starts, so changing it later does not reinterpret a run already under way. Under **When a built slice changes**, choose **Mark it out of date** or **Rebuild it automatically**. ### Predictive modeling and issue status sync [Section titled “Predictive modeling and issue status sync”](#predictive-modeling-and-issue-status-sync) **Enable predictive modeling** suggests what to model next as greyed-out ghost elements beside the + you were reaching for. Accepting one adds it; ignoring one costs nothing. app.cratis.studio ![The Predictive modeling tab of the Acme Sales application settings with the Enable predictive modeling switch](/_astro/application-settings-predictive-modeling.CmD__SIb_Z1AonmR.webp) Predictive modeling is opt-in per application. **Keep slices and their issues in step** works every few minutes: a done slice closes the GitHub, Jira or Linear issues it is linked to, a closed issue marks its slice done, and reopening either reopens the other. When both changed, the slice wins. Only issues in trackers connected under **Integrations** are kept in step. Turning it on changes nothing by itself. ### Choose the builder [Section titled “Choose the builder”](#choose-the-builder) On **Building**, choose which connected builder builds this application’s slices, or leave Studio to build. Builders belong to the organization: see [Approve builders](/organization/builders/). You can also choose how pull requests are made (**One per slice** or **One per feature**) and whether Studio or the builder names the branch and commit. app.cratis.studio ![The Building tab of Application Settings with the builder choice, repository handling and pull request options](/_astro/application-settings-building.C1n_Ora2_QtVz4.webp) Choose who builds this application's slices. ## Domain settings [Section titled “Domain settings”](#domain-settings) A domain has fewer settings. app.cratis.studio ![Domain Settings of the Sales domain with Repository, Reviewers and Dangerous tabs](/_astro/domain-settings.UieKUkHe_ZSvLge.webp) Domain Settings of the Sales domain. * **Repository**: the **Relative path** of the domain’s folder, and the resolved path from the working copy. Code generation refuses folders that overlap. * **Reviewers**: who reviews slices in this domain. * **Dangerous**: **Rename domain** or **Delete domain**. Deleting removes the domain itself; its event models, personas, concepts and brainstorming boards are not deleted but become unreachable. There is no tool to restore it. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/) * [Implementation modes](/deliver/implementation-modes/) * [Set up AI](/organization/ai-providers/) * [Publish and collect feedback](/deliver/publish-and-feedback/) * [Roles and permissions](/organization/roles-and-permissions/)

# Roles and permissions

> What a maintainer and a member can do in Cratis Studio, and how to make someone a maintainer.

Every person in your organization has one of two roles: **Maintainer** or **Member**. The role decides who can look after the organization itself. It does not decide who may model: both roles work on boards. ## What each role can do [Section titled “What each role can do”](#what-each-role-can-do) | | Maintainer | Member | | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- | ------------------------------ | | Open **Organization settings** and read every page | Yes | Yes | | Invite and remove people, assign seats | Yes | No | | Buy seats, change or cancel the subscription | Yes | No | | Set up AI providers, agents, the decision engine and conversation mode | Yes | No | | Connect integrations, webhooks, builders and the MCP credential | Yes | No | | Protect a project or lift its protection | Yes | No | | See and change every project | Yes | Opens every project to read it | When a member opens a page they cannot change, the page tells them. The Agents page, for example, says *You are not a maintainer of this organization, so the agents are read-only here.* Choosing a plan works the same way: the trial banner tells a member to *Ask your organization’s maintainer to choose a plan.* ## Make someone a maintainer [Section titled “Make someone a maintainer”](#make-someone-a-maintainer) You need to be a maintainer yourself. 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Users**. 2. Find the person in the list and open their **Actions**. 3. Select **Make maintainer**. Studio asks you to confirm: *Maintainers manage the organization: they invite and remove people, assign seats and can see and change every project.* 4. Confirm. The **Role** column now says **Maintainer**. app.cratis.studio ![The Users page of Organization settings with a Role column and an Actions column for each person](/_astro/settings-users.CUABBLwi_ZnzPYB.webp) The Role column shows who is a maintainer. Names and addresses are blurred. ## Roles inside the application you model [Section titled “Roles inside the application you model”](#roles-inside-the-application-you-model) Roles in this page are about your Studio organization. The roles of the application you are modeling, such as *SalesManager* in Acme Commerce, are a different thing: you define them in **Application Settings** on the **Roles** tab. See [Project and application settings](/organization/project-and-application-settings/). ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Invite people and manage members](/organization/invite-people/) * [Seats](/organization/seats/)

# Seats

> Give people a seat so they can use Cratis Studio, take it back when they leave, and see what happens to someone without one.

A person needs an assigned **seat** to use Studio. Your organization has a pool of seats; you hand them out to people and take them back when someone stops needing one. ## Where seats come from [Section titled “Where seats come from”](#where-seats-come-from) * Your **free trial** includes seats while it lasts. * Seats you **buy** under **Billing**. See [Billing and your subscription](/organization/billing-and-subscription/). * Seats that Cratis **grants** your organization. ## See how many are in use [Section titled “See how many are in use”](#see-how-many-are-in-use) On **Settings** > **Users** the counter reads, for example, *3 of 5 seats assigned*. A plan with unlimited seats reads *N seats assigned (unlimited)*. app.cratis.studio ![The Users page of Organization settings with the seat counter above the list of people](/_astro/settings-users.CUABBLwi_ZnzPYB.webp) The counter shows how many seats are assigned and how many there are. ## Assign a seat [Section titled “Assign a seat”](#assign-a-seat) You need to be a maintainer. 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Users**. 2. Open the person’s **Actions** and select **Assign seat**. 3. Studio says it *assigns the next available seat from your seat pool* to that person. Select **Assign**. If none is free, the dialog says *There are no more seats available - add seats from the Billing page.* ## Take a seat back [Section titled “Take a seat back”](#take-a-seat-back) Open the person’s **Actions**, select **Unassign seat** and confirm with **Unassign**. The seat goes back to the pool. The person cannot use Studio until a seat is assigned to them again. ## What a person without a seat sees [Section titled “What a person without a seat sees”](#what-a-person-without-a-seat-sees) When your organization has no license, a maintainer sees **You do not have a license** with **Purchase seats**, and a member sees *Contact your organization admin to get a license.* The same screen appears when a license has expired (**Your license has expired**), is suspended while a payment is outstanding, or the trial is over (**Your free trial has ended**: *Your organization and its work are kept. Choose a plan to continue.*). ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Invite people and manage members](/organization/invite-people/) * [Billing and your subscription](/organization/billing-and-subscription/)

# See what your organization uses

> Read the Usage page to see how much computing the work Studio did for your organization used, by kind of work and by project.

The **Usage** page shows what the work Studio did for your organization used: CPU time, memory and the tokens models consumed. It is there so you can see where the effort goes. It is for information only; no limit is applied from this page. ## Open it [Section titled “Open it”](#open-it) Open **Organization settings** and select **Usage**. app.cratis.studio ![The Usage page with totals at the top and a breakdown by project](/_astro/settings-usage.B3SAu39l_2r288n.webp) Usage by project for the chosen period. ## What you can read [Section titled “What you can read”](#what-you-can-read) * **Period**: the last 7, 30 or 90 days. * **Totals**: **CPU minutes**, **Memory GiB-minutes**, **Elapsed minutes** and **Pieces of work**. * **By kind of work**: *Specification run*, *Play session*, *Code generation*, *Source control session*, *Agent or model call* and *Agent implementation*. * **By project**: the same figures for each project. Work that belongs to no project is listed as *Not for a project*. Play sessions, specification runs and agent implementation runs are measured while they run. Work that could not be measured says **Not measured** instead of counting as zero. Only numbers are recorded; what the work was about is never recorded. ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Billing and your subscription](/organization/billing-and-subscription/) * [Set up AI](/organization/ai-providers/)

# Set up webhooks

> Tell another system when something changes on your boards, and keep an eye on whether the deliveries arrive.

A **webhook** lets another system react to what happens on your boards. Each time something you chose changes, Studio sends a signed message to an address you give it, and tries again with a growing delay when that fails. Use one to start a pipeline when a slice is ready, post to a chat, or keep another tool in step. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * You are a maintainer of the organization. * You have an `https` address on a public host that can receive the message. Addresses inside a private network and plain `http` are refused. ## Add a webhook [Section titled “Add a webhook”](#add-a-webhook) 1. Open **Organization settings** and select **Webhooks**. 2. Select **Add webhook**. The **Add a webhook** dialog opens. 3. Enter a **Name** for your own reference, such as *CI pipeline*. 4. Enter the **Address** Studio should post to. 5. Narrow what it listens to, or leave everything on **Any**: * **Board**: one board, or any board. * **Kinds of board**: which kinds of board to include. * **What changed**: the kinds of item, such as a slice or a comment. * **How it changed**: added, renamed, removed, moved, updated, status changed, commented or restored. 6. Select **Save**. 7. Copy the **Signing secret** now. It is shown only once. app.cratis.studio ![The Webhooks page with the Add webhook button](/_astro/settings-webhooks.L0Uzc_VE_ZRRnMm.webp) Each webhook is listed here, with its deliveries. Caution Store the signing secret where the receiving system can read it. The receiver uses it to check that a message really came from Studio. Never paste it into a chat or a ticket. A webhook only receives changes that happen after you add it. ## Check that deliveries arrive [Section titled “Check that deliveries arrive”](#check-that-deliveries-arrive) Select **Deliveries** on a webhook to see what was sent. Each one shows **Delivered**, **Retrying** (with the attempt and the time of the next one) or **Given up**. **Hide deliveries** closes the list. A new webhook says *Nothing has been delivered yet.* until something it listens to changes. ## Change, rotate or remove a webhook [Section titled “Change, rotate or remove a webhook”](#change-rotate-or-remove-a-webhook) * **Edit** changes the name, address or what it listens to. * **Rotate secret** signs deliveries with a new secret from now on. Give the new secret to the receiver straight away. * **Remove** stops all deliveries to it. ## Build the receiving end [Section titled “Build the receiving end”](#build-the-receiving-end) What a message looks like, how to check its signature and the retry schedule are in the [webhooks reference for developers](/developers/webhooks/). ## Related pages [Section titled “Related pages”](#related-pages) * [Webhooks reference](/developers/webhooks/) * [Connect integrations](/organization/integrations/)

# Reference and help

> Keyboard shortcuts, a glossary, the meaning of statuses and colors, and where to get help with Cratis Studio.

Look things up here: a shortcut, a word you have not met, the meaning of a color, or the way to reach the team. [Keyboard shortcuts ](/reference/keyboard-shortcuts/)Every shortcut in Studio, grouped by where it works. [Glossary ](/reference/glossary/)The terms you meet in Studio, in plain words. [Statuses and colors ](/reference/statuses-and-colors/)What slice statuses, sticky note colors, build statuses and issue states mean. [Get help ](/reference/get-help/)Report a problem, follow your reports, ask on Discord and read announcements. [Profile and appearance ](/reference/profile-and-appearance/)Your name, photo, sign-in methods, background and voice preferences. [Release notes ](/reference/release-notes/)Where to see what changed in Studio. [Legal ](/reference/legal/)The terms and the privacy policy. New to the ideas behind the board? Start with [Event modeling](/event-modeling/). New to Studio itself? Start with [Get started](/get-started/).

# Get help

> Report a problem to the Studio team, follow what happens to it, ask a question on Discord and read announcements.

Studio is in beta, so you will sometimes meet a rough edge. Here is how to reach the team, and how to hear back. ## Report an issue [Section titled “Report an issue”](#report-an-issue) Use this when something does not work, looks wrong or confuses you. 1. Select the flag button in the top bar on the right. Its tooltip is **Report Issue**. 2. Choose **Report an issue**. The **Report a problem** dialog opens. 3. Enter a **Summary**, then describe it under **What happened?** 4. Select **Send report**. ![The Report a problem dialog with a Summary field, a What happened box, two options about letting support know who you are, and the Send report button](/_astro/report-a-problem-dialog.7HWcK1Mh_1b6FNi.webp) *The dialog lists what is sent along: where you are, your Studio version and your recent requests.* Studio attaches where you are in the product, the version of Studio and your last few requests, so you only need to describe what happened. Do not include passwords or other secrets. Two options let you decide how much the team may do. Both are off until you turn them on: * **Let support know who I am**: support sees your name and email. Without it, they only see where the report came from. * **Let support sign in as me to look into this**: only for this report, and only for the time you choose. When an error message appears anywhere in Studio, it can offer **Report this problem**, which starts the same report with the details filled in. ## Follow your reports [Section titled “Follow your reports”](#follow-your-reports) Open the same flag menu and choose **My issues**. It lists what you have reported, each with its **State** and its **Last activity**; updates appear there as support works on them. Turn on **Show closed** to include finished reports, or select **Report a problem** to send another. The states are explained in [Statuses and colors](/reference/statuses-and-colors/#issue-statuses). ![The My issues panel with the Report a problem button, the Show closed switch and a list of reports with their Issue, State and Last activity](/_astro/my-issues.B_pIjZwp_ZPSy0c.webp) *A report you have just sent shows as **Reported**. The **State** changes as support works on it.* ## Ask on Discord [Section titled “Ask on Discord”](#ask-on-discord) Select the Discord button in the top bar, whose tooltip reads *Join our Discord to ask questions and get help from the Cratis team and other developers*. The Cratis community is there for questions, ideas and design discussions. Use Discord to ask how something works, and use **Report an issue** when something is broken. ## What’s new and announcements [Section titled “What’s new and announcements”](#whats-new-and-announcements) * **What’s new** opens by itself the first time you sign in after a release, so you can read what changed. Use the arrows to page back through earlier releases, or choose **Show all releases**. See [Release notes](/reference/release-notes/). * **Announcements** are messages that the Cratis team publishes to organizations. They appear in Studio, and you dismiss each with **Got it** or move on with **Next**. ## About [Section titled “About”](#about) Select the information button in the top bar to open **About**. It shows the **Version** and **Build** of Studio you are using, which helps when you report an issue, and a link to the **Release notes**. ![The About dialog showing the Studio logo, the version and build, and a Release notes link](/_astro/about-dialog.6cHyRor__Z1N8gVC.webp) *Quote the version and build when you report an issue.* ## Things only Cratis can do [Section titled “Things only Cratis can do”](#things-only-cratis-can-do) A few things are handled by the Cratis team rather than in Studio: granting seats, extending or ending a trial, and deleting an organization. For these, report an issue or contact Cratis. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Get started](/get-started/) for a first tour of Studio. * [Keyboard shortcuts](/reference/keyboard-shortcuts/) and the [glossary](/reference/glossary/) for quick lookups. * [Your organization](/organization/) for questions about people, seats and billing.

# Glossary

> The terms you meet in Cratis Studio, in alphabetical order, each explained in a sentence or two.

Words in the examples come from the Acme Commerce sample project. The ideas behind most of these terms are explained in [Event modeling](/event-modeling/). ## A [Section titled “A”](#a) ### Actor [Section titled “Actor”](#actor) Whoever does the work in a row of the board: a person using screens (**UI Actor**), the system acting on its own (**System Actor**) or an AI conversation (**Chat Actor**). See [Screens and personas](/event-modeling/screens-and-personas/). ### Agent [Section titled “Agent”](#agent) An AI teammate in your organization. Studio has a fixed roster: Atlas models, Merlin writes code, Quill writes documentation, Sherlock reviews, Echo carries the conversation, Hunch suggests what to model next, and Iris designs screens. A maintainer gives each agent a provider and a model. See [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/). ### Application [Section titled “Application”](#application) One system that a team builds, inside a [project](#project). An online shop is one application. Acme Commerce has five: Acme Catalog, Acme Shop, Acme Sales, Acme Purchasing and Acme Warehouse. See [Modules and features](/event-modeling/modules-and-features/). ### Automation [Section titled “Automation”](#automation) A [slice](#slice) where the system reacts to a fact and acts on it, with no person involved. For example, **EmptyBasketAfterCheckout** empties the basket once an order is placed. See [Automations and translations](/event-modeling/automations-and-translations/). ## B [Section titled “B”](#b) ### Blueprint [Section titled “Blueprint”](#blueprint) A ready-made starting point for an application’s screens, with templates you can use and a preview you can run with its own sample data. See [Design screens](/screens/). ### Brainstorming board [Section titled “Brainstorming board”](#brainstorming-board) A free-form board in a [domain](#domain) for the early, messy thinking: events written as facts, sticky notes, regions and chat bubbles. Events can be moved from it onto an [event model](#event-model). See [Discover your domain](/discover/). ### Builder [Section titled “Builder”](#builder) Software that turns modeled slices into code, on any machine that can reach Studio. A builder registers itself and builds nothing until a maintainer approves it. See [Build and deliver](/deliver/). ## C [Section titled “C”](#c) ### Command [Section titled “Command”](#command) Something a person or the system asks for. A command is an intent and can be refused. Commands are blue on the board. See [Commands, events and read models](/event-modeling/commands-events-and-read-models/). ### Concept [Section titled “Concept”](#concept) A typed value the business talks about, such as a quantity, an order number or an email address. A concept can carry rules that hold everywhere it is used, and can be marked as personal information. See [Discover your domain](/discover/). ### Constraint [Section titled “Constraint”](#constraint) A rule about what must never happen twice, such as two customer accounts with the same email address. See [Commands, events and read models](/event-modeling/commands-events-and-read-models/). ### Context map [Section titled “Context map”](#context-map) A diagram of an application’s domains and how they relate through the events they publish and consume. ## D [Section titled “D”](#d) ### Domain [Section titled “Domain”](#domain) One area of the business inside an application, such as Storefront or Warehouse. A domain owns the event models, brainstorming boards, personas, concepts, constraints and other canvases that describe that area. See [Modules and features](/event-modeling/modules-and-features/). ## E [Section titled “E”](#e) ### Event [Section titled “Event”](#event) A fact that happened, written in the past tense and never taken back. Events are orange on the board. See [Commands, events and read models](/event-modeling/commands-events-and-read-models/). ### Event model [Section titled “Event model”](#event-model) The board that describes one domain as a timeline, read left to right. See [Event modeling in Studio](/event-modeling/). ### Event source [Section titled “Event source”](#event-source) One kind of thing a domain keeps a history of, such as a basket or an order. An event source says what identifies each one and which [streams](#stream) it has. ## F [Section titled “F”](#f) ### Feature [Section titled “Feature”](#feature) A group of related behavior inside a [module](#module). The Shop module has the features Browse, Basket, Checkout and My orders. Features can nest. See [Modules and features](/event-modeling/modules-and-features/). ### Finding [Section titled “Finding”](#finding) A problem or gap that Studio points out on a board, such as a slice with no specification or a field that nothing fills. Each finding says why it matters and suggests a fix. ## G [Section titled “G”](#g) ### Given context [Section titled “Given context”](#given-context) A named starting situation that several [specifications](#specification) can share, either events that already happened or the state a read model is in. See [Specifications and Play](/event-modeling/specifications-and-play/). ### Given, When, Then [Section titled “Given, When, Then”](#given-when-then) The three parts of a [specification](#specification): what was already true, what happens, and what should be the result. See [Specifications and Play](/event-modeling/specifications-and-play/). ## I [Section titled “I”](#i) ### Implementation mode [Section titled “Implementation mode”](#implementation-mode) How an application’s modeled behavior becomes code. The four modes are **Screenplay only** (the model is the deliverable), **Static code**, **Static code + AI** and **AI only**. See [Build and deliver](/deliver/). ## L [Section titled “L”](#l) ### Lineage [Section titled “Lineage”](#lineage) The trail of where a value comes from. Select an element and ask **Where does this value come from?** to follow it back through read models, events and commands. ## M [Section titled “M”](#m) ### Maintainer [Section titled “Maintainer”](#maintainer) An organization role. Maintainers invite and remove people, assign seats, set up AI and integrations, and can see and change every project. See [Your organization](/organization/). ### MCP [Section titled “MCP”](#mcp) The Model Context Protocol, a standard way for an AI agent to work with a tool. Studio offers an MCP connection so that agents can read and change your organization’s models. See [For developers](/developers/). ### Mockup [Section titled “Mockup”](#mockup) A screen written as an HTML and CSS page and attached to a [screen](#screen). See [Design screens](/screens/). ### Module [Section titled “Module”](#module) A whole area of the business on a board. Modules hold [features](#feature). See [Modules and features](/event-modeling/modules-and-features/). ## O [Section titled “O”](#o) ### Organization [Section titled “Organization”](#organization) Your company or team in Studio. It holds the people, the AI setup, the integrations and the billing, and its projects never mix with another organization’s. See [Your organization](/organization/). ## P [Section titled “P”](#p) ### Persona [Section titled “Persona”](#persona) A description of one kind of person in a domain: who they are, what they want and which roles they hold. Personas can be given to actors, used in specifications and used in a preview. See [Screens and personas](/event-modeling/screens-and-personas/). ### Play [Section titled “Play”](#play) Runs an event model as if it were the finished application, in a sandbox. See [Specifications and Play](/event-modeling/specifications-and-play/) and [Play an event model](/check/play/). ### Policy [Section titled “Policy”](#policy) A named rule that says who may do what. It admits some callers and turns the rest away. Policies belong to the whole application. See [Screens and personas](/event-modeling/screens-and-personas/). ### Preview [Section titled “Preview”](#preview) Draws a screen with sample data, optionally as a particular persona, without running the whole model. See [Design screens](/screens/). ### Project [Section titled “Project”](#project) The top level of the work, typically one per team. Everything a team builds is inside a project. See [Modules and features](/event-modeling/modules-and-features/). ### Projection [Section titled “Projection”](#projection) The recipe that builds a [read model](#read-model) from events. When this event happens, change that value. See [Commands, events and read models](/event-modeling/commands-events-and-read-models/). ### Prologue [Section titled “Prologue”](#prologue) A way to capture an existing system, with a separate tool, so that Studio can help you work out a model of it. It records only names of tables, columns and endpoints, never the data. This feature is in beta. See [Discover your domain](/discover/). ### Public event [Section titled “Public event”](#public-event) An event that an application publishes as a contract for the rest of the company. See [Automations and translations](/event-modeling/automations-and-translations/). ## Q [Section titled “Q”](#q) ### Query [Section titled “Query”](#query) How a screen asks a read model a question, such as *the basket with this id*. See [Commands, events and read models](/event-modeling/commands-events-and-read-models/). ## R [Section titled “R”](#r) ### Read model [Section titled “Read model”](#read-model) What someone looks at afterwards, built from events. Read models are green on the board. See [Commands, events and read models](/event-modeling/commands-events-and-read-models/). ### Role [Section titled “Role”](#role) A named kind of access defined in an application, such as Shopper or SalesManager. Personas hold roles, and policies and specifications use them. Do not confuse it with an organization role, which is either Maintainer or Member. ## S [Section titled “S”](#s) ### Screen [Section titled “Screen”](#screen) What a person sees and clicks, attached to a slice. See [Screens and personas](/event-modeling/screens-and-personas/) and [Design screens](/screens/). ### Screenplay [Section titled “Screenplay”](#screenplay) The text format in which Studio can export and import an event model, in files that end in `.play`. See [Share and export](/share/). ### Seat [Section titled “Seat”](#seat) A license for one person to use Studio. A person needs an assigned seat to sign in and work. Maintainers assign and free seats. See [Your organization](/organization/). ### Seed data [Section titled “Seed data”](#seed-data) What an application starts with: named collections of starting events. Play and Preview use it as sample data. ### Sketch [Section titled “Sketch”](#sketch) A screen drawn with a pen, boxes and labels, attached to a [screen](#screen). See [Design screens](/screens/). ### Slice [Section titled “Slice”](#slice) One behavior of the system, drawn as one column on the board. The four types are **State Change**, **State View**, **Automation** and **Translator**. See [Slices](/event-modeling/slices/). ### Specification [Section titled “Specification”](#specification) A concrete example of a slice’s behavior, written as Given, When, Then. See [Specifications and Play](/event-modeling/specifications-and-play/) and [Check a slice with specifications](/check/specifications/). ### State change [Section titled “State change”](#state-change) A [slice](#slice) where a person asks for something and facts are recorded. See [Slices](/event-modeling/slices/). ### State machine [Section titled “State machine”](#state-machine) The stages that something goes through, such as placed, paid, packed and shipped, and the events that move it along. It follows a read model and never decides on its own. ### State view [Section titled “State view”](#state-view) A [slice](#slice) where a person looks at something built from earlier facts. See [Slices](/event-modeling/slices/). ### Sticky note [Section titled “Sticky note”](#sticky-note) A note on a board. Its color says what kind of note it is: yellow for a question, pink for a risk, green for a decision, blue for an assumption. See [Statuses and colors](/reference/statuses-and-colors/). ### Storyline [Section titled “Storyline”](#storyline) A named path through the board, such as a user journey, that you can step through and play. See [The event model board](/board/). ### Stream [Section titled “Stream”](#stream) A separate line of history within an [event source](#event-source). Every event source starts with one called Default. ### Suggestion [Section titled “Suggestion”](#suggestion) A slice, feature or module that Studio proposes for a feature. You preview it on the board and accept or dismiss it. See [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/). ## T [Section titled “T”](#t) ### Template [Section titled “Template”](#template) A frame for screens. A template chosen on a module or feature applies to the screens inside it, unless a screen chooses another. See [Design screens](/screens/). ### Translation [Section titled “Translation”](#translation) A [slice](#slice) that carries facts across the border between two areas, either bringing an outside fact in or publishing one of your own. Studio’s slice type is called **Translator**. See [Automations and translations](/event-modeling/automations-and-translations/). ## U [Section titled “U”](#u) ### UI profile [Section titled “UI profile”](#ui-profile) How an application looks and what its screens are built from: component libraries, styling and blueprints. Changes are a draft until you review and apply them. See [Design screens](/screens/). ## W [Section titled “W”](#w) ### Workflow [Section titled “Workflow”](#workflow) What happens step by step once something starts a run, with triggers, waits and approvals.

# Keyboard shortcuts

> Every keyboard shortcut in Cratis Studio, grouped by where it works, with the Mac and Windows variants.

On a Mac, **Cmd** is the command key and **Option** is the alt key. On Windows and Linux, use **Ctrl** where a Mac uses Cmd, and **Alt** where a Mac uses Option. Shortcuts that act on the board only work when you are not typing in a field. Typing always wins: when the cursor is in a text box, copy, paste and delete work on the text. ## Event model board [Section titled “Event model board”](#event-model-board) | Action | Mac | Windows and Linux | | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- | | Copy the selected slice, feature, module or event | Cmd + C | Ctrl + C | | Paste | Cmd + V | Ctrl + V | | Paste an event as a linked copy (events only) | Cmd + Shift + V | Ctrl + Shift + V | | Duplicate the selection | Cmd + D | Ctrl + D | | Select more than one element | Shift, Cmd or Ctrl + click | Shift or Ctrl + click | | Delete the selected sticky notes | Delete or Backspace | Delete or Backspace | | Zoom | Cmd + scroll, or pinch on a trackpad | Ctrl + scroll, or pinch on a trackpad | Copy, paste and duplicate are also in the board’s left toolbar, so you never need the keyboard for them. Slices paste into a slice or a feature, and features paste into a module. ## Brainstorming board and the other domain canvases [Section titled “Brainstorming board and the other domain canvases”](#brainstorming-board-and-the-other-domain-canvases) The domain canvases are the brainstorming board, concepts, constraints, personas, event sources, given contexts, seed data and policies. | Action | Mac | Windows and Linux | | ---------------------------------------- | -------------------------- | --------------------- | | Delete the selection | Delete or Backspace | Delete or Backspace | | Copy the selection (brainstorming board) | Cmd + C | Ctrl + C | | Paste (brainstorming board) | Cmd + V | Ctrl + V | | Select more than one item | Shift, Cmd or Ctrl + click | Shift or Ctrl + click | On the canvases other than the brainstorming board, Delete removes the selected sticky notes. A card is removed from its own header, where Studio can tell you what depends on it. ## Design canvas [Section titled “Design canvas”](#design-canvas) The design canvas holds all the screens of an application as artboards. | Action | Mac | Windows and Linux | | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------- | ----------------------- | | Select tool | V | V | | Add a note | N | N | | Add a region | R | R | | Add a chat bubble | C | C | | Undo | Cmd + Z | Ctrl + Z | | Redo | Cmd + Shift + Z | Ctrl + Shift + Z | | Go back to the select tool, close the open panel, or clear the selection | Esc | Esc | | Delete what is selected | Delete or Backspace | Delete or Backspace | | Zoom | Cmd + scroll, or pinch | Ctrl + scroll, or pinch | | Pan | Drag the empty canvas | Drag the empty canvas | | Rename a region | Double-click its name | Double-click its name | The letter keys only work without a modifier key, and not while you are typing. ## Screen editor [Section titled “Screen editor”](#screen-editor) These work while a component or one of its items is selected on a screen. | Action | Mac | Windows and Linux | | --------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------- | | Deselect (the item first, then the component) | Esc | Esc | | Rename the selected item | Enter | Enter | | Move the selected item earlier or later | Option + Arrow key | Alt + Arrow key | | Delete the selected component or item | Delete or Backspace | Delete or Backspace | | Go back from a screen you are peeking at | Esc | Esc | ## Preview [Section titled “Preview”](#preview) | Action | Mac | Windows and Linux | | ---------------- | --- | ----------------- | | Exit the preview | Esc | Esc | The same key leaves the blueprint gallery’s preview and goes back to the gallery. In the UI profile designer, **Enter** opens the selected page in the editor and **Esc** goes back. ## Board chat and voice [Section titled “Board chat and voice”](#board-chat-and-voice) | Action | Mac | Windows and Linux | | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ----------------- | | Start or end conversation mode | Cmd + Shift + . | Ctrl + Shift + . | | Say one line: hold to record, press both keys again to finish | Hold Shift + Option | Hold Shift + Alt | | Throw away a recording that is in progress | Esc | Esc | | Send a comment or a chat message | Enter | Enter | | New line in a message to an agent | Shift + Enter | Shift + Enter | Conversation mode is available only when your organization has allowed it and AI is set up. See [Profile and appearance](/reference/profile-and-appearance/) for how replies are spoken, and [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/) for the bigger picture. ## There is no shortcut for this [Section titled “There is no shortcut for this”](#there-is-no-shortcut-for-this) Studio has no keyboard shortcut for searching a whole board. To find something, use the pickers that search events, the **Storylines** tool, **Focus on a feature**, or ask the board chat. See [The event model board](/board/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [The event model board](/board/) * [Talk to your board](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/), for the chat bar and dictation * [Profile and appearance](/reference/profile-and-appearance/)

# Legal

> Where to read the Cratis Studio terms and conditions and privacy policy, and when you are asked to accept them.

Two documents govern your use of Cratis Studio: * The [Terms and Conditions](https://cratis.studio/terms-and-conditions.html) are the agreement between Cratis and your organization. * The [Privacy Policy](https://cratis.studio/privacy-policy.html) explains how personal data is collected, protected, kept and erased. Both are published on [cratis.studio](https://cratis.studio), and what the website shows is what you accept in Studio. ## When you are asked to accept them [Section titled “When you are asked to accept them”](#when-you-are-asked-to-accept-them) * **When you sign up.** Before an organization is created, you accept both documents. * **When you accept an invitation.** If you join an organization by invitation, you sign in, accept the documents and join. * **When your organization buys seats.** A maintainer ticks **I have read and accept the Terms and Conditions and the Privacy Policy** before the purchase can be completed. Each document opens in a window so that you can read it first. If one cannot be loaded, the window says so and suggests trying again or contacting support. ## When they change [Section titled “When they change”](#when-they-change) The two documents share one version, and they are always presented together. When the documents change, Studio tells every user of every organization by email, with the changed documents attached. Acceptances you have already given remain a record of what was agreed at the time. ## Questions [Section titled “Questions”](#questions) For questions about the documents themselves, contact Cratis. See [Get help](/reference/get-help/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Get started](/get-started/): signing up and the free trial. * [Your organization](/organization/): members, seats and billing. * [Billing and your subscription](/organization/billing-and-subscription/): where the terms are accepted on purchase.

# Profile and appearance

> Your name and photo, the ways you sign in, the background Studio draws behind your boards, and how agents speak to you.

Your account profile holds everything that is about you rather than about an organization. Open the account menu in the top bar and choose **Account profile**.
