Studio asks first, and you confirm by selecting **Yes** or typing yes. ## Replies and interruptions [Section titled “Replies and interruptions”](#replies-and-interruptions) Replies are short. An agent speaks the short form and the full answer stays in the chat. Start talking to interrupt it, or say “stop” to silence it without asking for anything new. Select **Speak replies** next to the microphone to turn spoken replies on or off. ## Voices and your preferences [Section titled “Voices and your preferences”](#voices-and-your-preferences) Each agent has its own voice. A maintainer changes it under **Settings** > **Agents**: edit the agent, choose a voice under **Voice** and select play to hear it. Under your profile, **Voice and conversation** holds your own settings (see [Your profile and appearance](/reference/profile-and-appearance/)): whether replies are spoken (also for typed lines), how quickly Studio treats a pause as the end of your turn, whether it keeps listening after your turn (with hands-free off, the microphone mutes after each turn), when a silent conversation ends, and how fast agents speak. app.cratis.studio ![The Voice and conversation tab of the account profile with Speak replies and two drop-down lists](/_astro/account-profile-voice-and-conversation.Cl9brvcu_QyRCA.webp) Your own voice and conversation preferences. ## Dictation and transcription [Section titled “Dictation and transcription”](#dictation-and-transcription) Dictation is a single line: hold **Shift+Option**, or select the microphone, and speak. A maintainer can also turn on **Clean up with a language model** under **Settings** > **Transcribing**, which tidies finished phrases with the provider and model they choose. When it is on, those phrases go to that AI provider. ## Your data [Section titled “Your data”](#your-data) * Your speech goes to Studio’s own speech service, which turns it into text and produces the spoken replies. It does not go to your organization’s AI provider. * The words that Studio sends on to an agent, and the parts of the board the agent needs, go to your organization’s AI provider, as when you type. * A spoken line that reaches the chat is stored like a typed line. Lines Studio ignores are not stored. * While the microphone is open, the indicator above the bar shows it. * A member holds one conversation at a time, and a conversation in which nobody finishes a sentence for 30 minutes ends on its own. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Talk to your board](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/) * [Suggestions and predictive modeling](/working-with-ai/suggestions/) * [Guided modeling and modeling by voice](/working-with-ai/guided-modeling-and-voice/) * [Conversation settings](/organization/conversation-settings/), for maintainers

# Explain and generate

> Ask an agent how a domain, event model, module or feature works, or have it generate a project, domain, event model, feature or slice from a description.

Two things save a team a lot of typing: asking an agent to **explain** a part of the model, and asking it to **generate** a first draft you can then edit. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) AI must be set up, and an agent must have a model. When it is not, Studio says: “No AI agent is configured.” See [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/). ## Explain how something works [Section titled “Explain how something works”](#explain-how-something-works) Use this when you open a board you did not build and want the story in words. 1. Find the thing you want explained: the domain, the event model, a module or a feature. 2. Open its menu and select **Explain how this domain works**, **Explain how this event model works**, **Explain how this module works** or **Explain how this feature works**. On a brainstorming board it is **Explain this board**. 3. Wait while Studio shows **Generating explanation…**. 4. Read the **How it works** panel. You can also ask from the chat bar, for example “Explain this board”. The short answer appears in the bar, and **Open the full explanation** shows the detailed one. See [Talk to your board](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/). If it fails, Studio shows the reason and **Report this problem**. Check that an AI provider is connected and an agent has a model. ## Generate content [Section titled “Generate content”](#generate-content) Use this to get a draft from a description. 1. Choose what to generate: * **Generate content with AI** on an event model. * **Generate content for module**, **Generate content for feature** or **Generate content for slice** from the matching menu. * **Generate Project with AI** or **Generate Domain with AI** to start a whole project or domain. 2. Describe what you want in the box (“Describe what you want to generate…”). On the home page the box asks “What are you building today?”. 3. Select **Generate**. While it works you can select **Cancel**, or **Show what the model is doing** to follow along. 4. Review the result on the board. Caution Generated content is a first draft. Read it, check it against your own knowledge of the business, and edit it. You can also run [specification checks](/check/) over what you keep. If a generation fails, **Report this error** sends the details along with a request and correlation id. ## What leaves Studio [Section titled “What leaves Studio”](#what-leaves-studio) Your description and the part of the model the agent needs go to the AI provider your organization chose. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Talk to your board](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/) * [Guided modeling and modeling by voice](/working-with-ai/guided-modeling-and-voice/) * [Suggestions and predictive modeling](/working-with-ai/suggestions/) * [Check the model](/board/check-the-model/)

# Guided modeling and modeling by voice

> Describe a system and let an agent walk you to a checked model, or talk a feature onto the board.

Two ways to model with an agent when you would rather talk than draw. **Guided** takes you from a description to a checked model, one step at a time. **By voice** places what you say onto a feature on the board. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) AI must be set up, and a modeling agent must be ready. Studio checks this when you open the dialog, and says if it cannot tell. See [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/). ## Start a guided session [Section titled “Start a guided session”](#start-a-guided-session) 1. On the board’s toolbar, select **Guided modeling and modeling by voice**. The **Model with an agent** dialog opens on the **Guided** tab. 2. Under **Start a guided session**, describe what the system does and who uses it. Or select **Upload a document** to give the agent a requirements document. Plain text and Markdown documents can be uploaded. 3. Select **Start**. 4. Work through the **Steps** the agent shows you, answering when it asks a question. The steps follow the phases of event modeling: **Discovery**, **Slices**, **Specifications** and **Review**. They begin with brainstorming events and roles and ordering them on a timeline, and go on to commands, read models and screens, automations and translations, features and slices, and Given/When/Then specifications. The last steps check completeness and validate the model. When the agent asks something, it shows **The agent asks** with a box for your **Answer**. At the end you get a **Verdict**: **Pass**, **Pass with warnings** or **Fail**. ## Model by voice [Section titled “Model by voice”](#model-by-voice) Use this when you can describe a process out loud and want it placed on the board. 1. In the **Model with an agent** dialog, open the **By voice** tab. 2. Under **Feature**, choose the feature to work on. If there is none, add a feature to the board first. 3. Select **Start listening** and talk through the process. 4. While the agent places what you describe, the dialog says **Adding to the board…**. Select **Stop listening** when you are done. If speech-to-text is not available, listening is off, and you can type what you would have said. If something could not be transcribed, try again or type it. ## What leaves Studio [Section titled “What leaves Studio”](#what-leaves-studio) Your description or uploaded document, and your answers, go to your organization’s AI provider, together with what the agent needs of the model. Your speech is turned into text by Studio’s own speech service before the words reach the agent. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Conversation mode](/working-with-ai/conversation-mode/), for talking with an agent about the whole board * [Talk to your board](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/) * [Check the model](/board/check-the-model/)

# Suggestions and predictive modeling

> Preview a slice Studio suggests, add it to the board whole, or discuss it with the modeling agent first.

When you are not sure what to model next, Studio can propose it. It looks at what is already on the board and suggests slices, features or modules. A [slice](/event-modeling/slices/) is one unit of behavior. A suggested slice comes complete: its command, its event and its read model with their properties, the projection that builds the read model from events, and its screen. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) AI must be set up, and the application must have **Enable AI** on. See [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/). ## See a suggestion before you take it [Section titled “See a suggestion before you take it”](#see-a-suggestion-before-you-take-it) 1. Find a suggested slice in a feature’s header. 2. Select **Preview**. The slice appears at the end of the feature, faded and outlined, exactly as it would be added. Nothing is saved while you look at it, and you can preview several suggestions at once. On the previewed slice’s header, select the checkmark to add it, or **×** to stop previewing it. If the slice needs an event the board does not have yet, its header warns you before you add it. ## Add a suggestion [Section titled “Add a suggestion”](#add-a-suggestion) Select the checkmark beside a suggestion to add it straight away, or the checkmark on its preview. Either way the whole slice is added, with its properties, projection and screen. When a part of a suggestion cannot be added, Studio says which part it left out, for example “Added Cancel Order, without its screen.”. A slice whose read model or trigger depends on an event that is not on the board is not added at all, and Studio tells you which event is missing. ## Use the chat bar [Section titled “Use the chat bar”](#use-the-chat-bar) You can also work with suggestions from the [chat bar](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/), typed or spoken. | Say | What happens | | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | | “Apply the Cancel Order suggestion” | The suggestion is added. | | “Preview the Cancel Order suggestion” | The suggested slice is shown on the board as a preview. | | “Tell me about the Cancel Order suggestion”, or “Why did you suggest Cancel Order?” | The modeling agent discusses it with you. | | “Apply it”, after discussing one | The suggestion you discussed is added. | You do not need the exact name. When the name could mean more than one suggestion, Studio asks which one you mean, and you answer with the name or “the first one”. When it is not sure, it asks “Do you mean Cancel Order?” before adding anything. ## Predictive modeling [Section titled “Predictive modeling”](#predictive-modeling) Predictive modeling goes one step further: as you reach for the **+** to add something, Studio shows ghost elements beside it, and accepting them adds them. Caution Predictive modeling is opt-in. Someone with the right to change the application’s settings turns on **Predictive modeling** for the application, and it needs the predictive modeling agent to be set up. To turn it on, open the application’s settings, select the **Predictive modeling** tab and switch on **Enable predictive modeling**. app.cratis.studio ![The Predictive modeling tab of the application settings with the Enable predictive modeling switch](/_astro/application-settings-predictive-modeling.CmD__SIb_Z1AonmR.webp) Predictive modeling is a setting of each application. ## Check that it worked [Section titled “Check that it worked”](#check-that-it-worked) The added slice is on the board like any slice you built yourself. Read it, change what does not fit, and run [your specification checks](/check/). ## What leaves Studio [Section titled “What leaves Studio”](#what-leaves-studio) Suggestions, and a discussion about one, are produced by the agent on your organization’s AI provider, from the part of the model it needs. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Talk to your board](/working-with-ai/talk-to-your-board/) * [Guided modeling and modeling by voice](/working-with-ai/guided-modeling-and-voice/) * [Application settings](/organization/project-and-application-settings/)

# Talk to your board

> Tell Studio what to change from the chat bar at the bottom of the board, and review what it did.

Every board has a chat bar at the bottom. Type what you want in plain words, and Studio changes the board, answers your question, or asks you a question back. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) AI must be set up for your organization, and an agent must be ready to run. If it is not, Studio tells you. See [Work with AI](/working-with-ai/). ## Make a change [Section titled “Make a change”](#make-a-change) 1. Select the chat bar. It says “Talk to your agents about this board, or tell Studio what to change…”. 2. Type what you want, for example “Add an event called Author Registered”. Press Enter. 3. Wait for **Working on your board…** to finish. The agent that did the work says what it did. app.cratis.studio ![The chat bar with the instruction typed but not sent](/_astro/chat-bar-instruction.UsgNEgvQ_Z2g8Fpw.webp) An instruction typed into the chat bar, ready to send. You can also dictate a line: hold **Shift+Option**, or select the microphone, and say it. For a longer, hands-free talk, see [Conversation mode](/working-with-ai/conversation-mode/). ## What Studio does with your line [Section titled “What Studio does with your line”](#what-studio-does-with-your-line) Studio works out what kind of line you wrote. | You write | What happens | | -------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | An instruction (“Add an event called Author Registered”) | Studio changes the board. | | An agent’s name first (“Merlin, can you…”) | That agent takes the line. | | A thought (“Maybe we need something for cancellations”) | Studio offers: “Want me to …?”. Say yes or no. | | A question about the board | The agent answers in a sentence or two. | | A request for an explanation | You get a short answer in the bar, and **Open the full explanation** shows the detailed one. See [Explain and generate](/working-with-ai/explain-and-generate/). | When Studio cannot tell what to do with a line, it says so and keeps your text in the box, so you can try again or rephrase it. When more than one thing could be meant, it asks “Which one do you mean? Select it on the board, or say its name.” ## Large changes are confirmed by you [Section titled “Large changes are confirmed by you”](#large-changes-are-confirmed-by-you) When a change is large or destructive, such as removing modules, Studio says what it will do and asks “Shall I go ahead?”. A spoken yes is not enough for these. Select **Yes**, or type yes and press Enter. ## Check that it worked [Section titled “Check that it worked”](#check-that-it-worked) Every change made from the chat bar is reported in the board’s chat, together with the line that caused it. Open the chat from the board’s toolbar to read the whole conversation. If a change is not what you meant, say “undo that”, or correct it in your next line. When an agent cannot decide something without guessing about your business, such as who approves a refund, it asks you one short question and waits. Answer it, or say “never mind”. ## Personas can speak in a brainstorming chat [Section titled “Personas can speak in a brainstorming chat”](#personas-can-speak-in-a-brainstorming-chat) On a brainstorming board, the chat has a **Speak as** menu above the conversation. It lists **Me** and the personas of the domain. Choose a persona, such as Buyer or WarehouseClerk, and your next message is written as that persona: the chat shows the persona’s name and initials, and you stay recorded as the author. Use it in a workshop to note down what each persona would say to the others. ## What leaves Studio [Section titled “What leaves Studio”](#what-leaves-studio) The text you type, plus the part of the board and of the board’s recent conversation that the agent needs to answer, go to the AI provider your organization chose. If you dictate a line, Studio’s speech service turns the speech into text first. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Explain and generate](/working-with-ai/explain-and-generate/) * [Suggestions and predictive modeling](/working-with-ai/suggestions/) * [Conversation mode](/working-with-ai/conversation-mode/) * [Keyboard shortcuts](/reference/keyboard-shortcuts/)
